#2960. AI is reshaping banking jobs – but its ability to drive profits remains unclear

Banks' third-quarter results have brought their use of artificial intelligence into sharper focus, as lenders automate routine work, reshape hiring and test whether productivity gains can translate into revenue growth.

This earnings season, Barclays unveiled layoffs in certain functions where AI is being deployed; while UniCredit and Santander said technology was freeing employees for higher-value work and improving processes. It comes as U.S. giants such as JPMorgan Chase and Goldman Sachs make similar changes in staffing and workflows.

But as banks pursue efficiency savings, strategists say evidence that AI is generating material, demonstrable revenue gains from it remains harder to pin down.

UniCredit CEO Andrea Orcel called AI an "opportunity to accelerate" that was allowing a shift toward more commercial, higher-value work and greater client engagement.

Orcel said the Italian lender — which reported its best-ever quarterly profit of 3.3 billion euros — is using the technology to "get us to the next level."

Orcel called AI "a lever that we didn't anticipate we would have, and working extremely well," on CNBC's "Squawk Box Europe" on Nov. 9.

Santander said AI initiatives generated 194 million euros in business value in the first nine months of 2026. AI is now being embedded in "all the processes that we are conducting at the bank," said Chief Financial Officer José García Cantera.

"AI basically helps, at least for now, decrease low value-added processes, and the company employees can then focus on more value-added processes — this affects both revenue and costs," Cantera told CNBC's "Squawk Box Europe" on Oct. 28.

"It's difficult to just to say how much comes from AI, but clearly AI is improving the quality of our services to clients, is helping us in asset quality, in cost management, and is having clearly a very positive impact."

Dutch lender ABN AMRO is using AI to support anti-money-laundering checks and expanding its partnership with Infosys to modernize its technology, while preserving human judgement, CEO Marguerite Bérard said in the bank's third-quarter results on Nov. 11.

"These improvements make the bank easier to run and scale, and help colleagues spend more time on activities that create value for clients," she said.

Some lenders announced big headcount reductions during the third quarter, which are "partly, but not solely, due to AI," said Andrew Stimpson, managing director and head of European banks research at KBW.

"The cuts have tended to come from back-office and support functions rather than the front office," Stimpson told CNBC via email.

Barclays said it plans to cut 5,000 jobs — about 5% of its workforce — in 2026. Roughly 60% of the reductions will affect support functions, including developer roles, the British lender said in its third-quarter earnings update, though it sought to emphasize that AI is not responsible for all the cuts.

The Financial Times reported on Oct. 7 that HSBC could cut around half of management and specialist roles, and as many as 70% of financial adviser roles, in its U.K. wealth business as AI reshapes how it serves clients.

Goldman Sachs said it intends to "constrain headcount growth" and cut a limited number of jobs. JPMorgan managers have been told to avoid hiring as the bank deploys AI across its businesses, CFO Jeremy Barnum told analysts in the lender's third-quarter earnings call.

A KBW study published in September examined AI use across European banks. Customer service — including chatbots and sorting customer requests — was the most frequently cited use case, followed by behind-the-scenes functions such as operations, identity checks and preventing financial crime.

Cost savings remain the most commonly cited goal: just under half of the use cases KBW tracked were aimed at cutting costs or improving efficiency. The report found banks are increasingly exploring ways to grow revenue, while noting AI is not yet seen as a major driver of profits.

Wealth management and advisory are emerging as key tests of whether AI can boost bank revenues, rather than only lower costs, analysts say.

Banks seem "fairly aligned" that they can do this in wealth management, where AI could enable advisors "to serve a larger number of clients as AI makes onboarding and reporting much more efficient," said Stimpson.

Sophia Whitbread, an analyst at Ninety One, said the evidence is still strongest for AI improving service, engagement and employee productivity, rather than demonstrably generating revenue. But initiatives that let advisors use AI to support more clients could expand access to personalized guidance without a corresponding increase in relationship managers.

She said wealth management was likely "the most important revenue test because AI can augment rather than replace the advisor."

"For European banks, the probable model is 'human adviser plus AI', especially at UBS, Standard Chartered and the private-banking units of Deutsche, ABN and the French banks, rather than a fully automated relationship," Whitbread said, pointing to JPMorgan's advisor and wealth-planning tools as a U.S. example of that approach.

AI is also reducing "traditional project management and back-office opportunities," according to Mark Astbury, a director at recruitment consultant Morgan McKinley.

There is greater demand for "specialists in AI, software engineering and data," Astbury told CNBC via email.

"London's financial services sector is increasingly becoming a technology employer. Demand for talent hasn't disappeared, but the skills banks are investing in are changing."

As AI takes on more routine tasks junior employees traditionally handle, "banks will need to rethink how they develop the next generation of talent," he said.

"We're already seeing senior roles demanding specialist AI skills that go well beyond basic use of tools like Copilot or Gemini, and I expect entry-level positions will become increasingly technical too."

#2959. If a Stock Market Crash Is Coming in 2027, Warren Buffett’s Advice Makes My Next Move Clear

Buffett’s long-standing advice offers a practical response to an uncertain market.

Volatility has returned to the stock market. Since hitting a record high on Oct. 6, 2026, the Nasdaq Composite has dropped more than 10%. That leaves many investors asking a rational, if concerning, question: Is a stock market crash on the horizon?

This anxiety leads many investors to pull up stakes and retreat from the market altogether. That strategy creates a new problem: when to get back into the market.

Therefore, I would suggest that during volatile periods – like right now – investors should take a page from Warren Buffett's playbook. Namely, build an investing strategy and schedule that can withstand volatility. Here's how to do it.

First things first, investors don't need to be fortune tellers. Indeed, even if someone could accurately predict market crashes, they would still need to accurately predict market bottoms – something that is just as unlikely as predicting tops.

A better strategy accepts that negative outcomes are inevitable, but rare, and can be mitigated with some planning.

To illustrate, think of someone who enjoys spending time outdoors but wants to avoid getting soaked by the rain. They could be 100% sure to avoid the rain by canceling every outdoor activity for the next year, but that would be a massive hassle. Conversely, they could simply check the weather forecast each morning and carry an umbrella on days when the chance of rain is high.

Once investors have planned for downturns, they can focus on which investments best carry them safely through volatile periods. Here, it's best to turn directly to the Oracle of Omaha for advice.

Rather than chasing short-term market fads, Buffett has advised investors to take long-term positions in real businesses they can easily understand. That's why Buffett's Berkshire Hathaway (BRKB -0.63%) (BRKA -0.48%) has owned large stakes in companies like Coca-Cola (KO +0.78%), American Express (AXP -0.67%), and Moody's (MCO +0.01%) for decades.

By stretching the time horizon out to 10, 20, 30 years or more, recent interest rate movements, unemployment reports, and GDP forecasts all fade in importance. Over long periods, what matters most is whether a company's business model is resilient and whether its management team is guiding it properly.

To borrow a line from Buffett himself, he quipped that he looks for companies so wonderful that "an idiot can run them, because sooner or later, one will."

Once you have the right mindset and the right stocks on your watch list, it's time to talk execution. A great strategy will still fail if it's not executed properly. Therefore, it's best to write rules that can be followed – even on a bad day.

Start small. If a person can only put $10 in the market each month, it's better than nothing. Raise the contribution amount when possible. Ensure there's an emergency reserve, separate from investment funds, for unexpected bills, large purchases, and similar expenses.

Finally, remember that investing during volatile periods can be emotionally taxing. Find ways to see the bright side when the market is moving lower. If a normal monthly contribution of $100 normally buys 1 share of stock, remind yourself that during volatile periods, $100 might buy 1.5 shares. Over time, those additional shares will add up.

No one knows what 2027 holds. A stock market crash could be on the way, but that won't keep anyone from investing. Instead, stick to the investing strategy and rules laid out by one of the best to ever do it: Contribute regularly, invest in great companies, and play the long game.

Over time, that strategy made Warren Buffett one of the wealthiest people on the planet, and it can no doubt help many others achieve their financial goals – regardless of whether a stock market crash is on the horizon.

#2958. Heating oil prices are up 59% from last winter. The spike is 'crushing folks,' Maine nonprofit says

Winter heating bills can strain household budgets. This year, a spike in heating oil prices could make the season especially difficult for households that depend on the fuel and have little financial wiggle room.

The national average price of heating oil was roughly $5.91 a gallon as of Nov. 9, up about $2.19, or 59%, from a year earlier, according to the U.S. Energy Information Administration.

In Maine, the average price per gallon is roughly $5.83, up about $2.49 from a year earlier, or about 75%. The state is among those whose residents may feel the price rise acutely — about 50% of homes there use heating oil.

The jump "is crushing folks," said Jason Parent, CEO of AVEC Community Action, a nonprofit agency based in Presque Isle, Maine, that provides heating assistance and other social services in three of the state's 16 counties. Many residents face immediate crises, he said, such as empty fuel tanks or reliance on unsafe heating sources.

"My fear is that there are households out there who have secondary fuel sources like wood stoves, pellet stoves and other things that are no longer operating and functioning in a safe way," Parent said.

The price surge comes on the heels of years of high overall inflation that has already squeezed household budgets, he said.

"The cost of living has made it impossible for households who had been figuring out how to get by without [financial help]," Parent said.

The price spike comes amid a tight global market for refined fuels, a category that includes heating oil and diesel. Supply shocks tied to the Iran war and the Russia-Ukraine war, low inventories and limited refining capacity have constrained supplies and added upward pressure on fuel prices, according to recent research from the Federal Reserve Bank of Kansas City.

The EIA predicts that a warmer winter in the Northeast will reduce consumption, helping to offset higher prices. In October, the agency forecast that the average household using heating oil will spend $2,115 from November through March, about 21% more than last year.

Meanwhile, homes primarily heated with natural gas are expected to spend an average of $640 this winter, down 9%, while those using propane are forecast to spend an average of $1,246, down 3%, according to the EIA's October forecast. Households using electricity for heating are expected to spend an average of $1,196, up 4% from a year ago. These are national figures.

Roughly 4.1 million U.S. households use heating oil as their primary means of heating their homes, according to the EIA. Most of them — close to 3.4 million — are located in the Northeast.

Unlike electricity or natural gas, heating oil is delivered in large quantities. Payment terms vary, but some dealers require a minimum delivery or charge extra for smaller orders. In Maine, for example, ordering at least 100 gallons for a standard 275-gallon tank can avoid a surcharge, according to the state attorney general's office. At current prices, that 100 gallons would cost about $600 — a tall order for some households.

AVEC's Home Energy Assistance Program, or HEAP, provides financial assistance to qualifying households, based primarily on income and the number of people living in the home. Funding originates with the federal Low Income Home Energy Assistance Program, or LIHEAP, which is run by the U.S. Health and Human Services Department and overseen by state agencies, often working with local organizations such as AVEC.

Maine and tribal grantees in the state received about $38 million from LIHEAP in October, according to a press release from outgoing Republican Sen. Susan Collins. AVEC administers about $4 million of that through HEAP, Parent said. AVEC also gets donations from community fundraising to help residents cover heating costs.

A component of HEAP is the Energy Crisis Intervention Program, which serves qualifying families who face an energy crisis, such as a potential electric shutoff or an empty oil tank and no financial means to replenish the supply.

AVEC began taking calls for that program on Nov. 2, Parent said.

"In the first hour alone, there were 150 voicemail messages on our emergency fuel line," Parent said. "People were in crisis before the program opened up."

Catherine Lafleche of Hersey, Maine, describes AVEC as a lifeline.

The 65-year-old is guardian to two grandchildren, ages 11 and 6, and is living mostly on about $1,100 in monthly Social Security benefits and state guardianship assistance, after shattering her upper-arm bone in a fall in April. Her injury required reverse shoulder replacement surgery, from which she continues to recover. Before that fall, she had worked as a substitute teacher and bookkeeper.

"I'm $6,000 short of income at this point in the year," Lafleche said.

Hersey is in Maine's northernmost county, Aroostook, where January and February bring average high temperatures in the 20s and average lows in the single digits, based on National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration data from 1991 to 2020 recorded at a Caribou Municipal Airport weather station. Last winter, the area experienced severe cold snaps, including on Jan. 24, when the high was minus 2 degrees Fahrenheit.

Lafleche applied to HEAP through AVEC in August, but a backlog meant potentially waiting until November for assistance. Unable to afford more fuel when it became chilly, she ran out of heating oil in early October and relied on a small wood stove to keep part of her house warm, burning Amazon boxes collected from other local residents because she didn't have enough firewood.

In late October, an AVEC representative called to say a charitable donation would cover 100 gallons of heating oil.

"When the oil man came, I cried," Lafleche said.

For residents in Maine who need help covering the cost to heat their homes and are seeking out help, "the need is outpacing the benefits," said Parent, of AVEC.

The average benefit through HEAP is about $750 per household this season, up from roughly $550 a year ago, Parent said. "That $750 is going less far than the $550 did last season" for heating oil, he said. "It's buying fewer gallons because of the precipitous increase."

The benefits are generally paid directly to the fuel supplier as a credit on the household's account. Even if a household qualifies, funding may run out.

"We're in a world of hurt now ... but it's going to be crisis time again in January or February," Parent said.

The Trump administration, in an effort to bring down heating oil prices, said it would release 300,000 barrels of heating oil from the Northeast Home Heating Oil Reserve, according to a Nov. 4 release from the U.S. Energy Department.

Whether adding this extra heating oil to the market will reduce prices for residents depends at least partly on how it gets doled out, said Jonathan Rubin, director of the Margaret Chase Smith Policy Center and a professor of economics at the University of Maine.

"No one really knows the economic impact, because will the home-heating oil distributors release it in one month, which would make it about 10% to 12% of a single month's supply? Or will they release it over the entire season, in which case it's only about one and a half percent of the entire season's home heating oil supply?" Rubin said.

"I would say that this is a modest short-term buffer," he said.

Congressional lawmakers have called for increased LIHEAP funding this year due to rising energy costs, including heating oil. Dozens of House lawmakers — mostly Democrats — signed an Oct. 14 letter to the House Appropriations Committee seeking an additional $3 billion in emergency supplemental funding for LIHEAP.

That followed an Oct. 1 letter from a group of Democratic senators and Sen. Bernie Sanders, I-Vt., to Robert F. Kennedy Jr., secretary of the Health and Human Services, also asking that he seek at least $3 billion more for LIHEAP.

Already, the government released roughly $3.63 billion in LIHEAP funding nationwide on Oct. 9 — 90% of the roughly $4 billion available under a continuing resolution that is currently funding federal agencies.

The timing for disbursing the remainder depends on when Congress either passes full-year funding for the 2027 fiscal year — which started Oct. 1 and runs through Sept. 30, 2027 — or passes another stopgap measure to keep the government running. If lawmakers do neither by Dec. 11, a federal shutdown would begin.

Whether the additional $3 billion requested for LIHEAP will be considered in any upcoming congressional appropriations process is uncertain.

Lafleche, the Hersey resident, said the 100 gallons of heating oil donated to her, along with some firewood she has now purchased, should keep her home warm until early December. She hopes to receive approval for benefits by then through HEAP, which has helped her in past years.

"We're not in this position by choice. Most of us made every plan to the contrary, but it stacked up to be not enough. And I feel like we don't deserve to be cold," she said.

#2957. Americas Gold and Silver: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

Galena's shift from manual mining to mechanized drilling trades near-term tons for infrastructure that builds future output, a tradeoff investors should weigh.

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 13, 2026, Americas Gold and Silver (USAS -6.02%) reported record quarterly silver production of over 800,000 ounces. Operating cash flow from low-cost silver mines funds critical underground modernization without heavy external financing. Three things management said stand out.

At the company's Galena complex mine in Idaho, management is replacing manual mining methods with mechanized long-hole drilling and updated equipment. This shift requires capital investments in shaft infrastructure and underground haulage, laying the base for sustainable future output.

Increased silver production and higher byproduct copper sales at the company's Cosalá operation in Mexico reduced cash costs (direct operational expenses per ounce of silver sold) to just over $14 per ounce. Cash generated at Cosalá provides organic funding for ongoing capital improvements across other assets.

Americas Gold and Silver eliminated its remaining metal delivery liabilities earlier in 2026. Removing these variable metal price obligations streamlines the capital structure, allowing future operating profit to flow straight to equity holders.

Industrial demand for silver and critical minerals is growing across advanced manufacturing and electrification. Americas Gold and Silver is advancing long-term mine life through paste fill technology (engineered backfill that stabilizes underground stopes) while maintaining strict development sequencing across its assets.

Management remains focused on disciplined capital execution to transform its underground operations into higher-margin silver producers.

#2956. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on the growth potential of these 3 stocks

Fears around the artificial intelligence trade and higher pressure on oil prices are weighing on global markets, but a few stocks offer investors solid growth prospects even in choppy times.

Investors with a long-term horizon can look beyond the ongoing volatility and track the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts to pick attractive stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Health insurer UnitedHealth delivered better-than-expected results for the third quarter of 2026. The company also raised its full-year earnings outlook, reflecting continued progress on its turnaround plan.

Following the Q3 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Erin Wright reiterated a buy rating on UNH stock with a price target of $529. "UNH's beat & raise reinforces turnaround momentum, with improving MA [Medicare Advantage]/Optum Health supporting a higher '26 EPS baseline & upside to '27 consensus," said Wright.

The five-star analyst added that better-than-expected performance in the UnitedHealthcare and Optum Health businesses supported another upgrade to the company's 2026 adjusted earnings per share outlook to at least $20.50. Wright noted that UNH raised the earnings guidance despite incremental investments planned for the fourth quarter of 2026.

Furthermore, Wright highlighted management's view that the consensus 2027 EPS of $22.63 was too conservative. Reaching even the low end of the company's 13% to 16% long-term EPS growth target would imply an EPS of $23.17.

Wright raised her 2026 EPS estimate to $20.65 from $19.82 and 2027 EPS to $23.24 from $22.54, reflecting the latest upside and an improving 2026 medical loss ratio to 87.4% from 88.1%. Overall, she believes that UNH's operational performance continues to improve, increasing her confidence in its UHC strategy and Optum Health turnaround execution.

Wright ranks No. 264 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 15%. See UnitedHealth Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Social media giant Meta Platforms has been making headlines since the launch of its Muse AI agent, which is seeing solid traction. Investors look forward to updates on the company's AI strategy when it announces its third-quarter results on Oct. 21.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Guggenheim analyst Michael Morris maintained a buy rating on Meta stock and increased his price target to $850 from $700, reflecting higher estimates for 2027. He expects Muse usage momentum and engagement levels to shape investor sentiment into the Q3 earnings call.

Specifically, Morris highlighted that, according to Apptopia, Muse reached 1.3 million daily active users in five weeks versus 300,000 in the first week. Given that Muse's current adoption represents less than 0.05% of Meta's 3.6 billion daily active user base, the analyst expects management to prioritize boosting usage over near-term monetization.

"Over time, we believe Muse monetizes primarily through businesses paying to reach consumers through their agents, consistent with the economics of Meta's core advertising business, rather than consumer wallets," said the five-star analyst. He added that the checkout integrations with Shopify and PayPal reflect this framework.

While there are concerns that a rival could overtake Muse or the recently launched Muse Wonder and push Meta into a prolonged cycle of overinvestment, Morris remains bullish due to the company's strong record in scaling WhatsApp and competing strongly with TikTok via Reels as well as free distribution to an extensive user base.

Morris ranks No. 629 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Meta Platforms KPIs on TipRanks.

Next is semiconductor company Marvell Technology. Earlier this month, Marvell held its Investor Day, where it raised its total addressable market estimate to $400 billion by 2030, reflecting solid AI-led demand tailwinds.

After hosting a non-deal roadshow with Marvell's senior management following the Investor Day, Stifel analyst Tore Svanberg reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $370.

The five-star analyst said that a major focus of the roadshow was the XPU and XPU Attach mix, as Marvell's Custom business expands toward its FY31 revenue target of $30 billion. In this regard, management reiterated that it expects a balanced mix of XPU and XPU Attach revenue in FY31, though it noted that either segment could grow rapidly enough to shift the mix toward 60/40 or 40/60.

While XPU drove most of the nearly $1.5 billion Custom revenue in FY26, Svanberg expects Marvell's XPU Attach business to grow substantially through the end of the decade, leading to a more balanced mix in this business. At the Investor Day event, the company raised its FY29 Custom target from more than $10 billion to over $12 billion, mainly driven by strength in the XPU Attach business.

Svanberg said that another key topic in the roadshow was whether Marvell can secure enough capacity to meet its raised targets. Management said that it met with suppliers ahead of Investor Day to share its five-year goals and received commitments to support these targets. The analyst believes that Marvell's supply chain commitments give it a competitive edge over rivals as customers seek reliable partners for large-scale infrastructure buildouts.

Svanberg ranks No. 91 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 31.8%. See Marvell Technology Statistics on TipRanks.

#2955. Lazio, mercato e rinnovi: Cancellieri attende il parere di Gattuso

Per Cataldi, Lazzari e Pellegrini si prospetta una partenza a gennaio o la liberazione a giugno.

Cataldi, Lazzari e Pellegrini sono i tre giocatori con contratto in scadenza nel 2027 che la Lazio considera in uscita: potrebbero partire a gennaio oppure liberarsi a giugno. Secondo quanto riportato da Corriere dello Sport, Diversa la posizione del quarto, Cancellieri, per il quale resta possibile una trattativa. In attesa del verdetto della commissione di vigilanza sui conti, previsto a inizio dicembre, il prossimo mercato rischia un nuovo embargo nonostante il primo posto della squadra.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Secondo il Corriere dello Sport, Lotito e Fabiani stanno dando priorità ai rinnovi, distinguendo le posizioni in scadenza nel 2027 da quelle del 2028.

Danilo Cataldi ha 32 anni ed è tornato alla Lazio dal prestito alla Fiorentina nell’estate in cui il mercato biancoceleste era bloccato. Palladino aveva lasciato il club viola, ma ne avrebbe chiesto la conferma; dopo la sua partenza è arrivato Pioli. Cataldi è rimasto e, per Sarri, la sua presenza è stata importante in una stagione segnata dall’operazione di Rovella per la pubalgia e dal mancato impiego di Belahyane.

Anche Cataldi si è operato a fine stagione ed è rientrato soltanto pochi giorni fa. Sulla sua posizione peserebbero soprattutto gli attriti passati con Lotito. Una possibile destinazione è il Bologna, oggi allenato da Palladino: il trasferimento potrebbe avvenire a gennaio o a giugno, con una scelta che spetta al centrocampista.

Lazzari compirà 33 anni il 29 novembre e veste la maglia della Lazio dal 2019. Nell’agosto 2025 era stato vicino alla partenza: lo voleva il Sassuolo, ma la società biancoceleste, con il mercato bloccato, aveva rifiutato per non ridurre le alternative sulla destra. In quel ruolo si è affermato Floriani, mentre Marusic è sotto contratto fino al 2028, con un’opzione per il 2029.

Il terzo nome tra i possibili partenti è Luca Pellegrini. Tagliato a settembre, è poi rientrato su spinta di Gattuso: dopo gli addii di Patric e Romagnoli sarebbe rimasto l’unico fuori. Sulla sua situazione incidono l’arrivo di Pedraza e un ingaggio da 2,4 milioni. La quota fissa è di 1,9 milioni; i bonus che portano alla cifra superiore sono legati a presenze facilmente raggiungibili. Finora ne ha collezionata una in Coppa Italia.

Pellegrini valuterà le possibilità di mercato a gennaio. In alternativa, potrebbe liberarsi a giugno. Per Cancellieri lo scenario è differente: la società aveva trovato una soluzione per venderlo nelle ultime battute del mercato estivo, ma Gattuso ne ha fermato il trasferimento perché non voleva restare senza un’alternativa sulla destra.

Lo cercavano Parma e Fiorentina. Era stata definita un’operazione che avrebbe portato Gudmundsson alla Lazio e Cancellieri alla Fiorentina, con altri 5 milioni destinati a Lotito. La società ha poi comunque concluso l’arrivo di Gudmundsson in prestito. Tra i giocatori in scadenza nel 2027, Cancellieri è l’unico con cui potrebbe essere avviato un tentativo di rinnovo: sarà decisivo il parere di Gattuso.

#2953. Is Sandisk (SNDK) Stock a Buy After Its 8-for-1 Split?

The split is a big one -- but it's not quite as meaningful as it seems.

On Oct. 29, Sandisk (SNDK -6.17%) announced that it would be splitting its stock 8-for-1. Whoever owned shares as of Nov. 9 will see their number of shares owned increase dramatically effective Nov. 19. Shares were recently trading around $885 apiece, pre-split.

If Sandisk has been on your radar, you might be wondering whether the stock will be a buy post-split. Here's my answer: Shares of Sandisk will be just as attractive (or not attractive) post-split as they were pre-split.

Here's a little stock-split math: Let's say that you own 10 shares pre-split, and that they're trading at $900 apiece just before the split. So pre-split, your shares are worth $9,000 (that's 10 times $900). Post-split, you'll have 80 shares (woo-hoo!) -- but at the same time that your number of shares is multiplied by 8, the share price is divided by 8. So post-split, you'll own 80 shares valued at $112.50 apiece (that's $900 divided by 8). Multiply your 80 shares by the new price of $112.50, and your stake's total value is... $9,000. See? Nothing really changed very much.

The company's revenue isn't increasing eight-fold. Its debt isn't shrinking by a factor of eight. Its business is pretty much the same, pre- and post-split.

Stock splits are mostly accounting events. They can make a stock seem cheaper, but remember that a $900 stock can be an undervalued bargain and a $9 stock can be an overvalued proposition, on its way to becoming a $2 stock.

So your question might be rephrased to just be: "Is Sandisk a buy now?" Well, consider that:

I think I'll be adding Sandisk to my watch list, as it seems like a promising opportunity.

#2952. Securitize Corp. (SECZ) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

First public quarter brings record tokenized volumes and a pending Rivera deal.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Securitize Corp. (SECZ -6.56%) reported its inaugural quarterly results as a public company, highlighting the expansion of its blockchain-based financial infrastructure through strategic integrations and product launches. Management reported record volumes of tokenized assets on the platform, attributing the growth to increased institutional adoption by major global asset managers.

The company entered into a definitive agreement to acquire Rivera, an asset-backed financing platform, to introduce credit origination capabilities and connect stablecoin capital with real-world assets. Leadership emphasized that its regulated broker-dealer and transfer agent licenses provide a permanent framework for trading tokenized equities that does not rely on temporary regulatory exemptions. The company also secured registration as an investment advisor and initiated a capital management program through a newly authorized share repurchase plan.

As we have mentioned before, the global equities market is enormous. it is estimated to be a $169 trillion and a very small portion is today represented on chain.

That tells us just how early this market remains. it is also important to recognize that not all tokenized equities are created equal. There are 3 fundamental different approaches. The first 1 is issuer sponsored tokenization. Where the token represents the actual share ownership is reflected in the issuer's shareholder register. Second is what are called custodial security entitlements where tokens that are backed 1-to-1 by underlying shares and holder have legally recognized entitlements. These are regulated under Article 8 of the UCC, fully legal and compliant in the US. And third, they are synthetic products that provide exposure to a surprise without the same ownership structure.

These are mostly offered offshore and in the current structure do not comply with US regulations. Our strategy focuses on the first 2 approaches. Issuer sponsored shares and bank security entitlements. Differentiation is not just the blockchain technology. it is the legal rights and regulated infrastructure underneath. We bring together transfer agency, broker dealer capabilities, trading infrastructure, access to issuer relationships. that is why we believe we are particularly well positioned as this market develops. As we mentioned before, tokenized stocks are at a very early stage. there is about $4.6 billion in the market represented by this chart, We will-- or they will break down a little bit in a second. As October 27.

That is roughly the level of stable points reached in March 2020. Since then, stablecoins grew substantially 64x as the utility increase This analogy shows what early stage adoption can look like. Predicting that tokenized stocks will follow the same path or growth of the same rate. The opportunity depends on real investor rights, trading access, liquidity and practical use cases for tokenized stocks. When we went public in New York Stock Exchange on July 2,, we decided to list our own shares on the Solana and Avalanche blockchain from the We were the first company doing this to showcase how tokenization is possible in the US in a regulated manner.

As of September 30 at the end of the quarter and during most of the Q3, SECZ was the largest individual tokenized stock. Approximately $278 million in value. Roughly, 0.6x the next largest stock represented on this chart, which is synthetic representation of another company. In our case, Continental, which is our transfer agent, provides the shareholder record infrastructure and we provide the tokenization capabilities. For the training portion of it, Jam helps provide the liquidity and extended hours tradings in the Solana ecosystem. We follow regulations related to pricing for trading providing always prices that are within the NBBO or national investment offer.

Therefore providing on chain the same pricing that you can have trading on a traditional broker dealer but on our website. SECZ is also accepted as collateral in non chain lending market, and we will be expanding the lending utility over time. We demonstrated that this issuer-sponsored model with our own public shares from first day of trading is possible in the US in a regulated manner. Subsequently, right after quarter end, we launched Securitize Stocks on October 8 with 12 US equities on Solana, another 13 more on November 2, bringing the offering to 25 of the top and most traded US stocks on chain.

Each token is fully packed 1:1 by underlying share, held for security entitlement under existing law, not a synthetic instrument. The structure is designed to preserve the applicable dividends, voting rights, and economic of the underlying shares. We also do not lend out the backing in shares, and we will allow investors to tokenize shares to lend themselves the shares, retaining most of the economics as opposed to what happens in the international markets where you are sure they are being lent out with broker dealers retaining most of the economics.

We also think that for retail investors being able to borrow against their shares is very important and something not easily available in the markets that you know, DeFi markets and blockchain infrastructure can bring to market. Our convertible security entitlement token model also creates a pathway for holders convert into issuer record shares when an issuer adopts a compatible structure in a seamless way without having to reissue the tokens or change anything on the investor end. USDC instant settlement and extended trading hours.

Includes safeguards around equivalent rights, issuer notice, and objections, and limits on eligible securities and trading bonds. The TSVs under the innovation exemption. First from our regulatory framework perspective, we operate with a fully registered broker dealer and ATS, and we have. The licenses are permanent to be able to operate without any expiration date. The innovation exemption, it is an exchange exemption. It allows people to operate with an exchange license. And expires in September 2031.

More important, what we do is fully regulated and therefore does not require issuer consent. it is not required for convertible and settlement tokens and only for issuer sponsored tokens that will require collaboration with the issuer and their transfer agent. However, the innovation exemption requires a 30 day issuer opt out. And given the exempted nature of the trading venue. You can see also here that there is another important thing in terms of different execution paths that you can choose under fully regulated broker dealer or the innovation exemption as well as how pricing works.

Alright. So let's move to the Rivera acquisition.

As we mentioned when we went public, 1 of the reasons we wanted to go public is because we think the market is going to consolidate and we wanted to be in a position of being able to acquire companies. But with our cash in our balance sheet as well as our liquid equity. So today, we are announcing the first plan acquisition of a company called Rivera that represents our entry into trade and asset creation opportunities beyond the tokenization and servicing business we have already built to date. We spend years building the infrastructure to bring financial assets on chain. First, through funds and more recently through public equities. We believe the next big opportunity is credit.

With our plan acquisition of Rivera, we are expanding into asset finance, a large market where stablecoin capital and blockchain infrastructure could create meaningful new opportunities. This is not simply about financing assets we already tokenize, but about expanding into a new asset class and participating in new parts of the value chain with a potential for additional revenue streams. Today, there are approximately $100 billion in stablecoin supply but very little of it is actually used to finance activity in the real economy. The same time, The US asset backed finance represents approximately trillion dollars market projected to grow to $9 trillion in 2030.

We see an opportunity to connect these 2 markets by directing stablecoin capital toward properly underwritten trade assets that finance businesses and real economy activity. This is natural next market for Securitize to pursue. Tokenization eliminate credit risk, but we believe it can help connect investors, originators, and funding sources in more efficient ways.

Rivera is a structured credit and asset backed finance business that works with fintechs originators, other capital intensive companies to access capital from the stablecoin markets Its team brings a specialized expertise in origination and structuring, underwriting using an AI engine and managing credit facilities including finances secured by receivables and other underlying assets in a programmatic way. that is compelling for us because Rivera brings both a new capability and an experienced team that will otherwise take significant time to build internally. What makes Rivera especially interesting is the combination of its credit expertise and its proprietary technology. They have 3 modules in their platform.

The first 1 called Argo supports underwriting and credit analysis using an in house AI underwriting platform that their credit team uses. The second 1 helps manage the financing facilities monitor covenants, and track performance in a more efficient way. And replaces 2 to 3 days of expected reviews with on chain infrastructure that is much more efficient Finally, provides a path to tokenize and distribute credit structures to secure this existing infrastructure Together, these capabilities could allow us to participate early in the life cycle of credit assets from origination and structuring through financing with stablecoins and on-chain distribution of, the tokenized credit products.

That creates opportunities for origination fees and participation in financing on our own balance sheet, with the potential for higher value monetization as the business develops. This is important. We are getting a team with deep expertise in asset backed finance and crypto technology. The strategic rationale is very straightforward. We build infrastructure to tokenize service and distribute assets. Rivera will expand our business into credit origination itself. #2951. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Global stock markets remain volatile amid persistent geopolitical tensions and concerns about the sustainability of AI trade. Given this backdrop, investors seeking steady income can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios.

Tracking recommendations from top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick attractive stocks, as these experts assess a company's ability to pay dividends consistently.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is a dividend king. The company has increased its dividend for 39 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share (annualized dividend of $7.12 per share), CVX offers a yield of 3.14%. Chevron will announce its third-quarter results on October 30.

In an earnings preview note on U.S. oil majors, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Chevron with a price target of $241. The analyst is focused on the company's international growth prospects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa, and the Eastern Mediterranean.

Specifically, Mehta highlighted management's commentary on solid execution in Venezuela, with gross production expected to more than double to nearly 600 kbd (kilo barrels per day) by 2031 via three joint ventures. He also noted a recent oil and gas condensate discovery in offshore Angola and is awaiting commercial updates on the West Qurna 2 and Nasiriyah projects in Iraq.

Furthermore, Mehta expects CVX to report improved productivity and capital efficiency across domestic shale assets. He expects the improvement mainly in the Permian Basin, where Chevron projects advanced chemical treatments to drive about a 10% recovery uplift in new wells and bring down capital spend per barrel by about 25% year-over-year. Overall, Mehta expects Chevron to report Q3 2026 EPS of $4.95, above the consensus of $4.89.

"While unfavorable timing effects and volatile commodity prices may present quarterly earnings headwinds, we highlight healthy U.S. refining margins and strong asset utilization as partial offsets," said Mehta.

Mehta ranks No. 381 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Chevron KPIs on TipRanks.

Kinetik Holdings (KNTK) is a fully integrated, pure-play, midstream company operating in the Delaware Basin. Earlier this month, the company declared a dividend of $0.81 per share, payable on October 30, 2026. With an annualized dividend of $3.24 per share, KNTK offers a yield of 5.82%. Kinetik will announce its Q3 earnings on November 4, with a conference call scheduled the next day.

Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, Goldman Sachs analyst John Mackay reiterated a buy rating on Kinetik stock and slightly raised his price target to $58 from $57. He expects the company to report Q3 2026 EBITDA of $279 million, higher than the Street's expectation of $270 million but lower than the second quarter's EBITDA of $281 million.

The 5-star analyst expects Kinetik's Q3 performance to gain from higher gas volumes after notable curtailments in the second quarter. Mackay also expects the quarter to benefit from higher production and steady commodity margins, even though some lower-margin contracts and weaker Waha spread marketing gains could weigh on results.

Overall, Mackay increased his Q3 2026 estimates to reflect higher volumes, driven by rapid Permian egress pipeline commissioning, stronger throughput at Kings Landing I, and higher M2M commodity prices, offset by increased costs.

"As natural gas takeaway constraints ease across the Permian, investor focus centers on KNTK's YE26 volume trajectory and durability of its multi-year growth," said Mackay.

Mackay ranks No. 569 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.9%. See Kinetik's Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Moving to Crescent Energy (CRGY), an exploration and production company with activities focused in the Eagle Ford, Permian, and Uinta Basins. With a quarterly dividend of $0.12 per share (annualized dividend of $0.48 per share), CRGY's dividend yield stands at 3.56%. Earlier this month, Crescent announced an agreement to acquire Eagle Ford assets from Devon Energy (DVN).

Heading into Q3 earnings on Nov. 2, Evercore analyst Stephen Richardson reiterated a buy rating on Crescent stock and slightly lowered the price target to $17 from $18, citing dilution from new shares.

"CRGY's recent acquisition of DVN's Eagle Ford assets marks a strategic, growth-oriented move, potentially enhancing cash flow performance," said Richardson.

The 5-star analyst noted that the acquisition of the Eagle Ford assets at a net purchase price of $3.85 billion will add about 68 MBoe/d of net production and over 600 net Tier 1 locations. Richardson added that a solid track record of synergies from past deals in the Eagle Ford region increases confidence about Crescent achieving efficiencies with this deal.

Additionally, Richardson noted that the financial implications of the acquisition seem impressive, with a projected increase in cash flow per share of about 9% for 2027 to 2029.

The analyst believes that Crescent is not just expanding its business through acquisitions but positioning itself as a more durable company, regardless of market conditions.

Richardson ranks No. 478 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Crescent Energy Statistics on TipRanks.

#2949. Lutnick faces questions over Nvidia spy scandal. His sons' firm has ties to the chipmaker

In late September, Cantor Fitzgerald hosted Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang and other top executives from the chipmaker for investor meetings in New York, according to a Cantor research report reviewed by CNBC.

The Wall Street firm liked what it heard.

"We continue to view NVDA as the best risk/reward in our coverage," Cantor analyst C.J. Muse and his colleagues wrote in a research report dated Oct. 3 that maintained a $350 price target and overweight rating on the stock.

Yet the firm, formerly run by Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick and now led by his sons, Brandon and Kyle, reported a nearly 50% decline in the number of Nvidia shares it held between September 2025 and March 2026, according to a CNBC analysis of securities filings that excludes options. Nvidia had been one of the largest stock positions reported in Cantor's filings.

The decline coincided with White House and U.S. intelligence officials pressing Lutnick to crack down on Megaspeed, a Singapore-based company suspected of funneling Nvidia's advanced artificial intelligence chips to China. Lutnick repeatedly resisted those efforts, Axios reported Nov. 11.

By the time Cantor hosted Huang in New York, Lutnick had also received a classified intelligence warning, according to a Nov. 10 Axios report, about Huang's ties to a suspected Chinese spy and had appeared alongside the CEO at a G20 technology summit in North Carolina.

Even as Cantor pared back its Nvidia stake, it continued doing business across the chipmaker's AI empire. A CNBC review of securities filings, corporate announcements and Wall Street research found the firm has helped arrange billions of dollars in financing for Nvidia's business partners while maintaining close ties to the chipmaker's leadership.

Those ties have drawn fresh scrutiny following Huang's resignation Thursday and Axios' reporting that Lutnick repeatedly resisted a crackdown on companies suspected of smuggling Nvidia chips to China, despite President Donald Trump authorizing new export restrictions on those companies for a third time on Nov. 5.

An ethics expert said the relationship between Lutnick and his former firm's investments may raise questions, given what the secretary knew about Nvidia.

Richard Painter, the former chief White House ethics lawyer for President George W. Bush and now a corporate law professor at the University of Minnesota, said that while "there's no proof of" Lutnick sharing nonpublic information with Cantor Fitzgerald, "the appearance doesn't look great."

"His conversations with his sons better be about publicly available information," Painter told CNBC.

There is no evidence that Lutnick's sons had advance knowledge of the intelligence concerns, that changes in Cantor's reported holdings were tied to any warnings or that the firm's business interests influenced their father's decisions.

"Cantor has to date from time to time had only immaterial stand-alone proprietary positions in Nvidia, unlike the positions reflected in 13F filings which were entered into in the ordinary course of business in connection with client swap transactions, that are common in institutional markets," a Cantor spokesperson told CNBC.

Cantor declined to answer CNBC's other questions. The Commerce Department, the White House, Howard Lutnick, Brandon Lutnick and Kyle Lutnick did not respond to CNBC's requests for comment or detailed questions about the findings.

As scrutiny of Huang intensified, Cantor Chairman Brandon Lutnick was publicly praising the chipmaker's leadership. "Nvidia is one of the great companies on the planet, and they have an amazing leadership team," Brandon Lutnick told Fox Business on Wednesday.

Trump is standing by Howard Lutnick. A White House official told Reuters on Thursday that the president maintains "complete confidence" in the commerce secretary.

But cracks in the administration's unity emerged Friday, when Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent told CNBC's "Squawk Box" that the departments of Treasury, Defense and State and the Office of the U.S. Trade Representative all "were in favor of putting Megaspeed on the entity list for months."

Bessent pointed to Commerce as the department responsible for imposing the entity list, which identifies restricted businesses and other groups, adding, "You will have to ask them about their internal processes."

Nvidia was Cantor's second-largest reported stock position before Howard Lutnick left Wall Street to become commerce secretary in February 2025, behind only bitcoin investor MicroStrategy, now known as Strategy. At the end of 2024, the firm disclosed more than 3.5 million Nvidia shares worth $479 million, according to its quarterly securities filing.

That share count held steady through September 2025, but its value climbed 36% to $652 million, according to Cantor's quarterly securities filing, as the Trump administration moved over the summer to ease restrictions on Nvidia's H20 chip sales to China.

Over the following six months, however, the number of Nvidia shares Cantor reported holding fell by 49%, to 3 million in December and 1.78 million by the end of March 2026, with their value dropping from $652 million to $310 million, according to a CNBC analysis of securities filings.

Pressure on Howard Lutnick was also mounting on Capitol Hill. Sens. Jim Banks, R-Ind., and Elizabeth Warren, D-Mass., in March urged the secretary to suspend or reconsider Nvidia's export licenses after three people linked to Nvidia customer Super Micro Computer were indicted on charges of conspiring to smuggle AI chips to China.

"The Trump administration, I think, leaks like a sieve when it comes to nonpublic government information getting to various hedge fund guys," Painter said, though he stressed there is no evidence Howard Lutnick had shared such information with his sons.

The filings do not establish why Cantor's reported share count fell or whether its overall financial exposure to Nvidia declined. Nvidia’s stock had more than doubled from its April 2025 lows by late October, before retreating in the following months. The stock fell about 6.5% between the end of September and the end of March.

Cantor's reported Nvidia holdings remained near that lower level through June 30, the latest quarter covered by public disclosures, at 1.81 million shares. Its next quarterly filing is due Monday.

Cantor has not publicly explained the decline.

Most of those shares were attributed to CF Secured, a Cantor brokerage subsidiary that uses stocks and derivatives to hedge customer transactions, according to its audited financial statements. Cantor also disclosed substantial Nvidia options positions during the time period CNBC examined, overwhelmingly skewed toward put options, which can protect against declines in the stock price.

As concerns about Huang escalated inside the Trump administration in May, according to the Nov. 11 Axios report, Cantor was helping finance the expansion of one of Nvidia's newest business partners, securities filings show.

On May 7, Nvidia and Australia-based cloud computing provider IREN announced a five-year, $3.4 billion cloud computing contract and a strategic partnership giving the chipmaker the right to invest up to $2.1 billion in IREN.

"That's not a passive financial investment. Nvidia is a partner who wins as we deliver," IREN co-CEO Daniel Roberts told investors on the company's quarterly earnings call that day.

Five days later, as Trump headed to China aboard Air Force One, Secretary of State Marco Rubio warned him about Huang's relationship with a suspected Chinese spy, The New York Times reported Tuesday. Huang, who had not initially been invited on the trip, joined the president's delegation during a refueling stop in Alaska.

Howard Lutnick had already been sent a highly classified intelligence memo detailing concerns about Huang's relationship, according to the Nov. 10 Axios report.

On May 12, the day Trump departed for China, IREN announced the pricing of a convertible bond offering that ultimately raised $3 billion when it closed two days later. Cantor was one of seven bookrunners on the deal, alongside JPMorgan Chase and Goldman Sachs.

On the day the offering closed, May 14, Lutnick's Commerce Department quietly cleared roughly 10 Chinese companies, including Alibaba and ByteDance, to buy Nvidia's advanced H200 chips, though some of those approvals predated the intelligence memo.

It is unclear when IREN hired Cantor for the offering or how much the firm earned from the deal. Cantor declined to answer CNBC's questions about its work with IREN.

Cantor reported a substantial position in IREN's stock.

By the end of June, Cantor reported $115 million in IREN shares and $362 million in Nvidia shares. Together, the two companies accounted for more than 9% of the value of Cantor's reported $5.2 billion in securities holdings, excluding options, according to CNBC's analysis of the filing.

IREN was not the only company in Nvidia's orbit that turned to Cantor for financing.

In October 2025, Cantor advised AI infrastructure developer Lancium on a $600 million financing deal to support its data center campus in Abilene, Texas, the first site of Stargate, the massive AI infrastructure project backed by OpenAI, SoftBank and Oracle.

Newmark, a commercial real estate firm controlled by Cantor, also helped finance the Abilene campus, arranging a $7.1 billion construction loan in May 2025.

Ten months later, Nvidia agreed to invest $2 billion for a roughly 20% stake in Lancium, with up to $1 billion more tied to future milestones, The Information reported in August. Lancium, which is also backed by Blackstone, confirmed an investment and a technology partnership on Aug. 24 without disclosing financial terms.

It's unclear whether Cantor continued advising Lancium after the financing deal or played a role in Nvidia's investment. Neither Cantor nor Lancium responded to questions about the investment.

Cantor's reported ties also extend to Nvidia's supply chain.

In May 2025, days after Howard Lutnick announced the transfer of his ownership stake in Cantor to his children via trusts, Semafor reported that bankers at the firm had informally advised executives at Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Co., which makes Nvidia's most advanced chips, on navigating Washington. Cantor called Semafor's report "baseless and false."

TSMC received billions of dollars in federal subsidies created during the Biden administration and administered by the Commerce Department to fund its U.S. manufacturing expansion.

Democratic senators called for an ethics investigation in December into whether the Lutnick family stood to benefit from Howard Lutnick's push to expand AI data centers.

And on Wednesday, Sens. Warren and Chris Coons, D-Del., demanded that Howard Lutnick, Rubio and White House chief of staff Stephen Miller preserve records on Nvidia's chip exports to China and intelligence warnings about Huang.

The senators questioned whether "prudent defensive counter-intelligence action was taken or national security recommendations were overruled."

"The place where I think the Trump administration has most failed here is in being persuaded by Jensen Huang and others to license the export of some advanced chips," Coons said Friday on CNBC's "Squawk Box," accusing the administration of turning a blind eye to the black market for more advanced chips.

#2947. SpaceX catches upper stage of Starship rocket in historic first

After a booster explosion on Friday, SpaceX successfully caught the upper stage, or spacecraft, portion of its massive Starship rocket from orbit on Saturday — a triumphant milestone for the company.

SpaceX made the historic catch using its "chopsticks," mechanical arms attached to a launch tower, at its facility in Starbase, Texas.

Test Flight 15, the most ambitious Starship test yet, marked the first time Elon Musk's company attempted to catch both the rocket's booster and upper stage.

Musk posted a video on X inspecting the upper stage craft after completing the capture.

During the first phase of the test flight on Friday, SpaceX failed to catch and recover the booster, instead diverting it to an offshore splashdown in the Gulf of Mexico where it exploded before hitting the water.

The rocket's upper stage then continued into orbit and flew around the Earth 14 times before successfully landing on SpaceX's launch tower, nicknamed Mechazilla.

The Hawaii Department of Transportation issued a temporary pause on some West Coast flights Friday, due to debris risk from the flight. Separately, the FAA issued a rare, nationwide ground stop due to telecommunications system failures. (Both of those orders have since been lifted.)

Making Starship a reliable and fully reusable rocket is critical to SpaceX's ambitions. In the near term, the company needs Starship to send more satellites into orbit to expand its Starlink satellite internet service, which is currently the company's profit center.

SpaceX is also expected to use Starship to bring astronauts and cargo from lunar orbit to the Moon's surface and back for NASA. Musk also has a grand vision to use Starship to "colonize Mars" and "make humanity multi-planetary."

During Test Flight 15 on Friday, SpaceX released another 26 of its newest Starlink v3 satellites into orbit, growing its commercial satellite internet network.

The company's constellation already included over 11,000 satellites in low Earth orbit. One Starship rocket can carry up to 60 Starlink v3 satellites, the company has said.

#2946. Ecopetrol: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

Ecopetrol is pausing LNG purchases when prices spike, leaving its refining edge and disciplined capital choices as the test for long-term holders.

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 13, 2026, Ecopetrol (EC -2.72%) reported an 86% year-over-year increase in net income. The energy business relies on refining flexibility to cushion declines in mature crude fields. Three takeaways from management explain where the company is headed.

Refining margin reached $45.6 per barrel during the third quarter of 2026, providing a buffer against maturing domestic oil fields. This operational strength generated higher profits while planned maintenance was underway.

The acquisition of Brava Energia gives the company a growth footprint outside Colombia. Managing the asset requires integrating financial planning while prioritizing return on capital over volume growth.

Global market price spikes caused by tensions in the Middle East prompted the company to pause LNG import purchases for its regasification facilities. Management prioritizes profitability over securing spot volumes under unfavorable commercial terms.

Operational strategy pivots to asset integration and capital discipline as mature domestic fields decline. The corporate portfolio relies on refining flexibility, offshore gas potential, and international expansion to drive future value while executives watch capital allocation discipline.

Investment governance demands strict returns above capital costs for all new projects. Strengthening execution across upstream and downstream segments establishes a foundation for long-term production stability.

#2944. How Rockstar's 'GTA VI' release could reshape gaming, streaming and even paid time off

It's been 13 years since the last installment of Grand Theft Auto, and fans are getting ready for what analysts say could be the biggest video game launch of all time.

The latest game in the franchise, "Grand Theft Auto VI," is set to launch on Nov. 19, after Take-Two Interactive's Rockstar Games delayed its launch twice. Roth analyst Eric Handler estimated the game maker spent more than $1 billion developing the game.

Sales figures could end up breaking records. Sensor Tower estimated over 12 million preorders through Nov. 13, and Morgan Stanley forecast the game could sell 40 million copies by March 2027.

Beyond launch week, the Grand Theft Auto, or GTA, franchise has a track record of lasting engagement over many years. Even 13 years after launch, "Grand Theft Auto V" is one of the top five most viewed games on Twitch this year.

In anticipation of the game's launch, investors have been taking note: Take-two's stock has more than doubled since the beginning of 2023.

The release is seen as not only a monumental moment for developer Rockstar Games and parent company Take-Two, but also a massive opportunity for the entire gaming industry.

Take-Two CEO Strauss Zelnick addressed demand in a recent "Squawk Box" interview, saying, "We tend to be pretty judicious when we talk about this sort of thing, but our expectations were high and they are being vastly exceeded so far."

A creator who goes by the name Deansocool, who has 375,000 followers on Twitch and posts content to YouTube and other social platforms as well, said "GTA VI" will enable creators to grow their audience and provide a massive opportunity for newer streamers to make a name for themselves.

"This game is going to make a lot of millionaires in 2027. A lot of creators gotta understand this is it. This is that golden ticket. This is like a hundred times bigger than Fortnite," he said in an interview with CNBC.

"It goes beyond this launch moment into this sustaining opportunity for them to grow, build, engage and retain their audience and ultimately support themselves by making money," said Twitch Chief Product Officer Mike Minton.

The release of the latest Grand Theft Auto title could draw so much attention that its impact will be felt beyond just the world of gaming.

BambooHR, an HR software platform where workers manage time-off, reported that paid time off requests for the "GTA VI" release date are nearly 13% higher than equivalent days in 2024 and 2025. It also noted that nearly one in five workers are planning to take time off for the launch.

PwC issued a report examining all the different sectors of the economy that could benefit from the game's launch — or be hurt by it. The firm projected that food delivery companies could see a surge from hunkered-down gamers, and Miami tourism could benefit. PwC noted that foreign visitors to Seoul's Bukchon Hanok Village, which inspired a location in the hit Netflix movie KPop Demon Hunters, increased 118% in the four weeks following the movie's debut.

Music streaming also looks set to get a boost. Tom Petty's "Love Is a Long Road," which appeared in the first "GTA VI" trailer in December 2023, reportedly saw Spotify streams jump by more than 37,000%.

On the downside, the game could distract from movies and streaming entertainment.

"How are people spending Thanksgiving? There's a lot of sports on TV, but you've also got a pretty solid group of your demographics that are going to be focused on playing 'GTA VI,'" said Bart Spiegel, who leads PwC's Global Entertainment & Media sector. "You're going to start to pull eyeballs away from video streaming platforms.

"I think you're going to hear a lot of analyst calls in the first quarter of 2027 saying, 'Hey, listen, some of our numbers were down because of the release of this game,'" Spiegel added.

Rockstar Games declined CNBC's interview request. Watch the video above to learn more.

#2942. Is It Too Late to Buy the Vanguard Growth ETF? Here's What History Says.

The Vanguard Growth ETF (VUG -1.08%) has unquestionably been one of the market's best performers over the past several years. But some investors might look at recent returns and wonder if it's too late to buy.

Even though the fund is currently about 9% below its recent high, its 21.2% average annual total return over the past three years and 17.4% average over the past decade will have some people feeling like they missed out on the bull market.

History suggests that buying after big rallies isn't necessarily a bad idea. The challenge around buying the fund right now, however, has nothing to do with performance.

A lot of people think that buying stocks at all-time highs means that there's no real value opportunity. In reality, however, stocks are usually near all-time highs because economic conditions are good and company performance has been solid. That's the kind of thing that can keep pushing prices higher, even if they're at highs right now.

A JPMorgan Chase (JPM -0.17%) report from 2024 found that forward-looking returns for the S&P 500 were actually slightly better when investing at all-time highs than they were when investing at any other time. That's not to say that you should push all in when stock prices are at all-time highs. But buying at highs historically hasn't posed the risks many investors think it does. A follow-up report in 2025 found no significant advantage or disadvantage to investing at all-time highs compared to any other day.

The Vanguard Growth ETF has roughly 130 to 140 stocks, but it's far from diversified. It currently has 72% of its portfolio in tech stocks. 63% of the portfolio is in just the top 10 holdings, with Nvidia (NVDA -0.95%), Microsoft (MSFT -0.85%), and Apple (AAPL -0.21%) accounting for more than half of that.

It's not necessarily the fund's fault. After all, it's just tracking a rules-based index, and the tech sector is where the growth is coming from right now. However, the concentration makes it vulnerable to a sharp pullback like the one we've seen over the past few weeks.

The practical path is to keep everything automatic. If you're making contributions monthly, keep making them. Don't try to time the market or sell because you think the bottom is going to fall out. If you're a long-term investor, your focus should be on wealth creation, not finding the optimal entry point.

You can also pair it with a total market fund, a value fund, or one with international stocks. These have different compositions that can help offset some of the more severe downside risks.

Either way, don't judge a fund based on its past performance. Look at what it can do for your future.

#2940. Energous: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

Enterprise customers are moving past pilots toward 500-store rollouts, but whether that converts into recurring software revenue remains the open question for Energous.

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 12, 2026, Energous (WATT -4.38%) announced that third-quarter revenue expanded to $3.4 million. Long-term investors need to see whether the business can convert initial trial programs into recurring software sales while restoring gross margin. Three things management said stand out.

Energous faced margin pressure earlier in 2026 due to manufacturing retooling and component costs. Price increases implemented in July lifted gross margin to 14% in the third quarter, and management targets historical hardware margins in the mid-30% range as offshore contract manufacturers that handle third-party assembly reach volume production.

Large enterprise customers in retail and e-commerce are moving past initial proof-of-concept testing, which evaluates whether the technology functions in real-world store environments. A major retail customer plans to expand coverage across 500 superstores and 150 warehouse club locations, which could accelerate Energous's transmitter hardware shipments.

Hardware sales provide initial adoption, but long-term enterprise value depends on software monetization. Energous is building out its e-Compass software platform to convert physical sensor readings into operational insights, establishing a foundation for recurring software-as-a-service subscriptions.

Revenue growth accelerated, and manufacturing operations stabilized across both contract assembly facilities. Energous held $28.8 million in cash at quarter end, providing runway to execute commercial rollouts without issuing equity. Management is tracking proof-of-concept conversions, customer expansion velocity, and gross margin progression toward its 40% long-term target.

Long-term expansion relies on embedding wireless power hardware as essential physical data infrastructure for enterprise automation.

#2939. $180 million Van Gogh tests the art market and collector confidence

A version of this article appeared in CNBC's Inside Wealth newsletter, a weekly guide to the high-net-worth investor and consumer. Sign up to receive future editions straight to your inbox.

The art auctions in New York next week could top $2 billion, as a treasure trove of masterpieces comes to the market for the first time in decades.

Total sales at Sotheby's, Christie's and Phillips are expected to be around $2.4 billion, which would equal last year's total, according to ArtTactic. Sotheby's alone is expected to pull in over $1.1 billion, with a single collection from The Blaquier-Arrieta Collection estimated at $450 million.

The collection includes the two big headliners of the week: Vincent van Gogh's landscape "Châtaigniers en fleurs," (Chestnut Trees in Flower) estimated at $180 million and Paul Cézanne's "Arlequin," estimated at $120 million. Neither has been seen in public for a half a century.

Sotheby's CEO Charles Stewart said demand is strong in the art market, especially for the highest quality blue-chip works, as rising stocks markets and private wealth boost the confidence of collectors.

"There's been enormous wealth creation and a lot of confidence in markets," Stewart said. "It's a global phenomenon. We're seeing recovery and greater interest across Asia and something really exciting happening from the Middle East across to Singapore and Europe and the U.S."

Christie's star of the week is Wassily Kandinsky's "Betonte Ecken," estimated at $60 million to $80 million. Christie's CEO Bonnie Brennan describes the market is "confident but discerning, rewarding truly exceptional works." She said recent gyrations in the stock market and fears of an AI bubble aren't likely to deter serious collectors.

"At the very highest end of our market, collectors tend to take a much longer-term view," Brennan said. "They understand that the opportunity to acquire a masterpiece may come only once in a generation, and that can be far more important than what the stock market has done in any particular week. In uncertain moments, the very best can actually become even more compelling."

The art market has continued to rebound this year after declines in 2023 and 2024, with auction sales in the first half up 70% over last year, to $6.8 billion, according to Bank of America.

Drew Watson, head of art services at Bank of America, said blue-chip art is outperforming, with Impressionist & Modern sales up 133% in the first half.

"Our outlook for the art market this November is cautiously optimistic," Watson said. "Improving liquidity, combined with an unusually strong concentration of fresh-to-market private collections, creates a favorable backdrop for the marquee sales."

#2936. Robinhood shares fall as crypto slump weighs on profits, offsetting gains in new businesses

Robinhood shares were down around 8% in pre-market trading on Wednesday as investors digested weaker-than-expected third-quarter earnings despite growth in options trading and prediction markets.

Here's how the brokerage performed in its quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with Wall Street estimates, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Despite that gain, transaction-based revenue of $867 million came in below Wall Street estimates from StreetAccount of $879 million, putting focus on whether Robinhood can sustain momentum across its trading businesses and reduce its reliance on volatile market activity.

Robinhood has been diversifying its business as it tries to expand its brokerage app into a broader financial services platform. Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Transaction-based revenue grew 19% from the same period last year to $867 million, driven primarily by a more than 500% increase in event contracts revenue. Additionally, options revenue grew 31% to $399 million and equities revenue rose 41% to $121 million.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

Chief Financial Officer Shiv Verma told CNBC event contracts on crypto movements did well in the quarter, offering investors another way to express a view on crypto despite the slump in prices and spot trading.

Net deposits on the Robinhood platform totaled $15.7 billion for the quarter, equal to a 17% annualized growth rate based on total platform assets at the end of Q2 2026. Over the past year, net deposits totaled $71 billion, representing 21% growth compared with total platform assets at the end of Q3 2025.
